DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
A solitary dot continuously travels forward through a looping circular tunnel. Tapping toggles the central gravity, pulling the dot inward or pushing it outward, letting the player drift between inner and outer rings to thread through gaps.

## Player
The player controls a single glowing dot that automatically moves along the tunnel’s forward direction. Its only actions are toggling gravity (inward vs outward) via tap or click.

## Core loop
The dot advances automatically around the tunnel while the player watches upcoming gaps. Each time a gap approaches, the player decides whether to pull the dot toward the center or push it outward, then taps to switch gravity. Successful navigation through a gap yields a small reward and the tunnel continues; mistimed switches cause collision with a ring segment.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Rotating ring segments**: Fixed arcs that leave gaps; colliding with a solid segment ends the game.
- **Moving barriers**: Short radial spikes that travel along the tunnel and require quick gravity switches to avoid.
- **Laser arcs**: Pulsing beams that appear on either the inner or outer ring, demanding the dot stay on the opposite side.

## Score
Score increments each time the dot successfully passes through a gap, with bonus points for consecutive gap passes without a miss. Collectible “energy pulses” appear occasionally; grabbing one adds a score burst.

## Win and lose
The player loses when the dot collides with any solid ring segment, barrier, or laser. On loss the screen shows “Game Over” with the final score and a prompt to restart. The game has no explicit win condition; instead high scores encourage replay, but a “perfect run” (clearing a preset number of gaps) triggers a “You Win!” celebration.

## Feel
A rhythmically pulsing, neon‑glow aesthetic with a steady yet tense tempo. The visual style is dark with bright cyan and magenta rings, and the audio is a minimal synth pulse that rises with each successful switch. Gameplay feels tight and reactive, rewarding precise timing.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of instantly switching gravity to slip through tight, moving gaps creates a satisfying “just‑in‑time” moment that makes players grin or gasp with each successful drift.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold & meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button and matching `meta.json` per CONVENTIONS.
2. Game state objects — declare plain objects for player, hazards, score, and phase with initial values at top of the script.
3. Animation loop — implement `startLoop()` with cancelAnimationFrame handling, try/catch wrapper, and clear canvas each frame.
4. Input handling — set up a `keys` Set via keydown/keyup, map gravity toggle, R-key restart, and restart button click to `reset()`.
5. Player movement — apply dt‑based physics to move the dot forward and adjust radial position based on current gravity direction.
6. Hazard system — define hazard data structure, spawn rules respecting `SAFE_RADIUS`, and dt‑scaled motion.
7. Collision detection — check player against hazards each frame, transition to `gameover` state on hit.
8. Scoring — increase score on successful gap passes, render the score at the top‑left of the canvas.
9. Rendering — clear canvas, draw background, tunnel rings, hazards, player (high contrast), and HUD each frame.
10. Game‑over overlay — dim the scene and draw “Game Over”, final score, and restart instructions.
11. Observable contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with `state`, `score`, player position/visibility, and an objective sentence.
12. Audio — add a lazy‑initialized `AudioContext` with a `beep()` helper and play sounds on hit, score, and death events.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Radial Gravity Switch</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Navigate a dot through a circular tunnel by toggling central gravity. Switch between pulling inward and pushing outward to drift between inner and outer rings and thread through gaps.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap or click to toggle gravity</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const CANVAS_W = 800;
  const CANVAS_H = 600;
const ANGULAR_SPEED = 1.5; // radians per second
const GRAVITY_ACCEL = 200; // radial acceleration (px/s²)
const RADIUS_INNER = 150; // inner tunnel radius
const RADIUS_OUTER = 250; // outer tunnel radius
let gravityDirection = -1; // -1 pulls inward, +1 pushes outward

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
  const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');

  let keys;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;
  let state = 'menu'; // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"
  let score = 0;

  // === RESET ==============================================================
  function reset() {
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    state = 'menu';
    score = 0;
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  // === LOOP ==============================================================
  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        // placeholder update/render steps
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error('loop error', err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  // No game logic yet.

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = '#0a0a0f';
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
  canvas.addEventListener('click', () => {
    // placeholder for toggle gravity on tap/click
  });

  // === AUDIO (lazy) ======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = 'square';
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain);
      gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start();
      osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: state,
      score: score,
      player: {
        x: 0,
        y: 0,
        visible: true,
      },
      objective: 'Navigate a dot through a circular tunnel by toggling central gravity.',
    };
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. ### Update function:

Replace placeholder comment with logic described.

Add code:

```
function update(dt) {
  if (state !== 'playing') return;

  // Update player angle
  player.angle += ANGULAR_SPEED * dt;
  // keep angle within 0..2π
  if (player.angle > Math.PI * 2) player.angle -= Math.PI * 2;

  // Update radial velocity and position
  player.vr += GRAVITY_ACCEL * gravityDirection * dt;
  player.radius += player.vr * dt;

  // Clamp radius within tunnel
  if (player.radius < RADIUS_INNER) {
    player.radius = RADIUS_INNER;
    player.vr = 0;
  } else if (player.radius > RADIUS_OUTER) {
    player.radius = RADIUS_OUTER;
    player.vr = 0;
  }

  // Spawn hazards
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnTimer = 0.6 + Math.random() * 0.8; // 0.6-1.4 sec
    const lane = Math.random() < 0.5 ? 'inner' : 'outer';
    hazards.push({ angle: Math.PI * 2, lane });
  }

  // Update hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.angle -= ANGULAR_SPEED * dt;
    if (h.angle <= 0) {
      // hazard reached player
      const playerLane = player.radius < LANE_MID ? 'inner' : 'outer';
      if (h.lane === playerLane) {
        // collision
        state = 'gameover';
        if (score > (window.__game?.highScore || 0)) {
          // optional highScore
        }
        beep(110, 0.25);
      } else {
        // safe passage
        score += 1;
        beep(880, 0.05);
      }
      // remove hazard
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}
```

We need to call update(dt) in startLoop before render.

Currently startLoop's placeholder comment says // placeholder update/render steps. We'll modify to call update(dt) before render.

### Render function:

Add drawing:

After clearing canvas, draw tunnel rings: maybe draw inner and outer circles with subtle colors.

```
function render() {
  const cx = canvas.width / 2;
  const cy = canvas.height / 2;

  // clear
  ctx.fillStyle = '#0a0a0f';
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

  // draw tunnel (inner and outer circles)
  ctx.strokeStyle = '#7df9ff';
  ctx.lineWidth = 2;
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(cx, cy, RADIUS_INNER, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.stroke();
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(cx, cy, RADIUS_OUTER, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.stroke();

  // draw hazards
  for (const h of hazards) {
    const r = h.lane === 'inner'
      ? (RADIUS_INNER + LANE_MID) / 2
      : (LANE_MID + RADIUS_OUTER) / 2;
    const hx = cx + Math.cos(h.angle) * r;
    const hy = cy + Math.sin(h.angle) * r;
    ctx.fillStyle = h.lane === 'inner' ? '#ff52ae' : '#ff8c52';
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(hx, hy, 6, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();
  }

  // draw player
  const px = cx + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius;
  const py = cy + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius;
  ctx.fillStyle = '#ffff00';
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(px, py, 6, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();

  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
  ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
  ctx.textAlign = 'left';
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);

  // Game over overlay
  if (state === 'gameover') {
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(10,10,15,0.7)';
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.textAlign = 'center';
    ctx.font = '44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
    ctx.fillText('Game Over', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
    ctx.font = '20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
    ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
    ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
    ctx.fillStyle = '#a5a5b8';
    ctx.fillText('Press R or click Restart', canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
  }
}
```

prompt (13,891 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:radial-gravity-switch:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. Failure feeds the error report back to you for correction.\n185: \n186: 1. Both `index.html` and `meta.json` exist.\n187: 2. `meta.json` parses as JSON and matches the schema above.\n188: 3. `index.html` parses, has exactly one `<canvas id=\"game\">`, exactly one `<button id=\"restart\">` after it, and the `<header>` text matches `meta.json`.\n189: 4. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`. (A game where neither happens has no progress signal — broken even if it runs.)\n197: \n198: ## Tips\n199: \n200: - Keep the implementation simple and robust over flashy and fragile.\n201: - Test mentally: imagine loading the page, pressing keys, watching for 5 seconds — would anything throw?\n202: - Use `requestAnimationFrame` for the loop.   keys = new Set();

  player.theta = 0;

We'll replace the placeholder comment with code:

function update(dt) {
   if (phase !== 'playing') return;
   // advance angular position
   player.theta += ANGULAR_SPEED * dt;
   if (player.theta > Math.PI * 2) player.theta -= Math.PI * 2;
   // apply gravity
   player.vr += gravityDirection * GRAVITY_ACCEL * dt;
   player.r += player.vr * dt;
   // clamp radius
   if (player.r < RADIUS_INNER) { player.r = RADIUS_INNER; player.vr = 0; }
   if (player.r > RADIUS_OUTER) { player.r = RADIUS_OUTER; player.vr = 0; }
   // compute screen position
   const cx = CANVAS_W/2;
   const cy = CANVAS_H/2;
   player.x = cx + player.r * Math.cos(player.theta);
   player.y = cy + player.r * Math.sin(player.theta);
}

function reset() {
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    // init player
    player.theta = 0;
    player.r = (RADIUS_INNER + RADIUS_OUTER) / 2;

function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== 'playing') return; // don't update if not playing

  // Update player angular position: always moving forward (clockwise perhaps)
  player.angle += ANGULAR_SPEED * dt; // rad/s
  // Keep angle in range 0-2π
  if (player.angle > Math.PI * 2) player.angle -= Math.PI * 2;

  // Update radial speed by gravity acceleration
  player.radialSpeed += gravityDirection * GRAVITY_ACCEL * dt;

  // Update radius
  player.radius += player.radialSpeed * dt;

  // Clamp radius between inner and outer bounds and bounce/kill? For now just clamp and reset radial speed maybe.

  if (player.radius < RADIUS_INNER) {
    player.radius = RADIUS_INNER;
    player.radialSpeed = 0; // stop at inner wall
  } else if (player.radius > RADIUS_OUTER) {
    player.radius = RADIUS_OUTER;
    player.radialSpeed = 0;
  }

  // Convert polar to Cartesian for rendering
  // The center of tunnel at canvas center
  const cx = canvas.width / 2;
  const cy = canvas.height / 2;
  player.x = cx + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius;
  player.y = cy + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius;
}

prompt (5,180 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Radial Gravity Switch
Concept: The dot auto‑advances through a circular tunnel; tapping toggles a central gravity that either pulls the dot inward or pushes it outward, letting you drift between inner and outer rings to thread through gaps.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
